**Book 188:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas I* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-875-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 1 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (Fase A outcomes and ATP, printed pp. 1–14) plus one guide section per student chapter, with objectives, lesson flow and timing, activities, misconception notes, remedial and enrichment tasks, project rubrics, answer keys keyed to named student-book pages, and reproducible ten-frame, number-bond and game templates (printed pp. 1–200). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. The guide reproduces the student book's chapter covers, placeholder ISBN and all; its own ISBN is as given here. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 189:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 190:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas III* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Hobri, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-936-3. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 191:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Adding zero — adding to zero and adding zero (Тэг дээр нэмэх, тэгийг нэмэх): 0 + 2, 0 + 1, 2 + 0; practice 3+0, 6+0, 0+1, 9+0… | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 47 |
| Addition tables / grids — practice sets within 10; addition grid completed across and down; number-card addition games on a 0+1…9+1 board | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 48 |
| Mixed practice within 10 — mixed sets (6+1, 7−6, 10−7, 5−5…); subtraction grids across and down; make three subtractions from three cards (3, 7, 4); tangram picture | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 58 |
| Self-test within 10 — self-test: part-whole with 6, joining pencils, taking ice creams away, difference by matching squares, bird/sheep pictures, 5−3, 3+7, 10−2, 2+8, add/subtract grid | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 59 |
| Number puzzles / games — place numbers 1-10 in a circle frame so the additions work (largest number in the red circle, each used once); board game moving on squares labelled with sums and differences within 10 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 60 |
| Doubles — sums of equal addends (Ижил нэмэгдэхүүний нийлбэр): pairs of fruit, socks, hands; 1+1=2, 2+2, 3+3, 4+4, 5+5; using bonds to compute 4+6, 3+4, 2+5 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 66 |
| Mixed add/subtract chains — mixed chain from a picture (books taken off and put back): 5 − 2 + 3 on a ten-frame; sets 8−5+2, 9−5+2, 10−4+1…; caterpillar chain starting at 2 (+2, +3, −1, +2, −3, +4, −0) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 68 |
| Word totals in groups — write and solve a mixed sentence for chicks joining/leaving a pen; two-step arrow pairs on two-row bars; totals of grouped clothes (4+2+3) and apples (2+5+3) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 70 |
| Making ten to add past ten — add/subtract numbers to twenty (chapter opener): cars in a car park as ten-frame + strip; 9 + 5 by splitting 5 = 1 + 4, 9 + 1 = 10, 10 + 4; write 12, 15, 14, 19, 16, 17 as 10 + ones | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 79 |
| Making ten — 9 + 4, 8 + 3 (8 + 2 = 10, 10 + 1), 7 + 6 on ten-frames with split diagrams; flow diagrams 7 + 5 (via 3 to make 10, then +2 = 12) and 9 + 3 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 80 |
| Matching sums to 20 — match ger pictures (6+5, 9+3, 8+5, 8+6, 7+6, 9+9) to their satellite-dish answers 11-18; in each aquarium colour two fish whose numbers total 18, 20, 17, 19 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 81 |
| Adding without crossing ten — make up a problem for goats joining a pen; 11 + 3 by splitting 11 = 10 + 1 (1 + 3 = 4, 10 + 4); 15 + 2 and 14 + 4 in split diagrams | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 82 |
| Commutativity past ten — books on shelves: 8 + 3 = 11 and 3 + 8 = 11, so 8 + 3 = 3 + 8; complete 6 + 4 = 4 + …, 7 + 2 = 2 + …; continue dot patterns | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 84 |
| Completing 20 — strips that make 20: 15 + 5, 13 + 7, 12 + □…; choose two bags of carrots that total 20 for the rabbits; missing addends □ + 4 = 20, 12 + □ = 20, 9 + □ = 20 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 85 |
| Fact families — car/butterfly diagrams: 10 − 6 = 4 with 6, 4 swapped roles; 14 − 8 = 6; complete 13 − 5 = 8 and 16 − 9 diagrams; table of totals split into two parts (12 = 5 + 7, 20 = 10 + …, 17 = 10 + …) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 90 |
| Sorting expressions / addition table — sort expression cards under their answers 7, 11, 14, 18, 20; complete a + table (rows and columns 1-10, 4 + 5 = 9 shown) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 91 |
| Self-test to 20 — self-test: books 7 + 7, 9 + 5 by making ten, 15 − 7 by splitting, ice creams taken away, comparisons 15 □ 13, triangles part-whole, pairs making 10, match 15+5, 5+9, 8+7 to answers | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 94 |
| Number-line games to 20 — consolidation: dice roll added to 7 and to 9 on a 0-20 number line; spinner (1-9) subtracted from 15 and from 18 on the line | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 95 |
| Adding whole tens — add/subtract (within 100): 30 + 20 as 3 tens + 2 tens on ten-strips, by +10 jumps 30 → 40 → 50 and on a 30-50 number line; pay prices 50, 70, 40 with pictured 10₮/20₮ notes; sets 30+20, 20+40, 50+20, 80+10… | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 110 |
| Two-digit + whole tens — 38 + 20 by splitting (38 = 30 + 8; 30 + 20 = 50; 50 + 8) and by +10 jumps; sets 14+30, 65+20, 30+33…; word problems: Sarnai has 35₮, Bold 40₮ — together how much? Grandfather has 43 lambs and 30 kids — how many animals?; commutativity 34 + 20 = 20 + 34 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 112 |
| Tens board game — race game on a cross-shaped board of tens (10-90) played with expression cards like 10 + 30 and two-coloured counters; first to the apple wins | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 116 |
| Doubles to 20 — find the doubles on the + table's yellow diagonal (1+1 … 10+10) and match them to 2, 4, …, 20; complete sharing word problems (two children share … pencils equally…); split sets of 8 and 10 dots into two equal parts; which numbers were coloured blue and which red — complete the colouring (even/odd colouring of 2-20) | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 126 |
| Two-digit + one-digit, no regrouping — add/subtract within a hundred (Зуу дотор нэмэх, хасах): 23 + 5 by splitting (23 = 20 + 3; 3 + 5 = 8; 20 + 8 = 28), on ten-strips and counting on 23 → 28 on a 20-30 line; 31 + 3, 22 + 7; sets 20+7, 16+3, 33+5, 45+2…; count the young animals in a pen picture, brown lambs against white lambs how many fewer | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 132 |
| Word problems and tables — Tuya and Tsogt made toys for their kindergarten siblings ("I made 14 toys", "mine are 3") — who made more, by how many, how many in all?; subtraction tables (− 3, 2 / − 5, 6 / − 0, 4 over 26, 47, 15…); a dice game on the even-number table 2-20 / 12-20 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 134 |
| Two-digit + two-digit, no regrouping — 17 + 21 by splitting both (7 + 1 = 8; 10 + 20 = 30; 30 + 8 = 38) and by +10 +10 +1 on a number line; 23 + 22, 35 + 11 in split diagrams; sets 14+25, 31+17, 42+15…; sequence +10: 11, 21, 31, … | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 135 |
| Consolidation games — add a dice roll to 14, 31, 33, 52; complete add/subtract grids; a 4×4 arrangement puzzle: place 1, 5, 10, 20 so every row, column and the red diagonal has four numbers summing to 36 | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 138 |
| Doubles letter puzzles — answer-behind-a-letter puzzles: complete the halves (10 = 5 + …, 14 = 7 + …, 16 = … + 8, 20 = … + 10, 12 = 6 + …, 18 = 9 + …) to spell what the fox is called; doubles 10+10=20, 15+15, 11+11, 9+9, 20+20, 13+13, 25+25, 18+18, 17+17 to spell what the boy likes | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 150 |
| Revision: chains and comparisons — write and evaluate expressions (sum of 46 and 21; decrease 37 by 14 in difference terms…); observe 17, 58, 67, 21, 45, 78, 28, 36, 41, 73 — name those ending in a/б digits, smaller than a given; from a 0-10 table find pairs with difference 2, pairs with sum 10, and the sum of the bigger and smaller neighbours of 4 | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 6 |
| Revision: chains to 100 — evaluate chains (14+10, 37−20, 52+20, 78−40, 56+21+12, 25+32−13, 46−22+14, 67−32−4); place the given numbers 2-7 on a train so each wagon's load totals 14; complete a hundred-chart fragment (56, 58… 87, 88, 89, 90, 100); find expressions equal to each row's first number (20 = 13+7, 24−4, 19+2? …); order sets of numbers with < signs (38, 41, 39, 40 descending…) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 7 |
| Adding a one-digit number with regrouping — add/subtract within 100: word problems set up as expressions (first-graders read 37 books, second-graders 8 more — in all? 37 + 8; third-graders 56, second-graders 9 fewer — difference 56 − 9); 37 + 8 by completing the ten: (37 + 3) + 5 = 45 with base-ten blocks; 56 − 9 = 40 + (16 − 9) = 47 | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 12 |
| Bridging methods — observe how 14 + 8 was found (split 8 = 6 + 2: (14 + 6) + 2 = 22) and how 24 − 9 was found ((20 − 9) + 4 = 15) with bundles of ten pencils; add/subtract pictured tens of pencils (17 + □, 36 − □) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 12 |
| Practice and commutativity in measures — complete 46 + 7 = (46 + 4) + □ and 62 − 6 = 50 + (□ − 6) from block diagrams; sums of block-pictured numbers; two ribbons 6 cm and 10 cm joined: the boy adds 10 + 6 = 16 cm, the girl 6 + 10 = 16 cm — both answers the same (the sum does not change when addends swap places); sets 17+5, 73+8, 66+9, 45+40, 30+68 and 17−9, 33−6, 75−7, 41−5, 48−30, 98−60 | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 13 |
| Two-digit + two-digit with regrouping — explain the seller word problems (37 + 26 pens over two days; 56 − 37 pencils left): 37 + 26 = (30 + 20) + (7 + 6) = 63 with blocks and a split layout ("37 is 30 and 7, 26 is 20 and 6; 50 and 13 make 63"); 56 − 37 = (40 − 30) + (16 − 7) = 19 ("10 and 9 make 19"); find sums of block-pictured pairs | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 14 |
| Hundred-chart method — two parallel exercise lists (headed sums / differences) over the same eight expressions 16+25, 47+24, 66+29, 18+23, 52−6, 74−15, 82−23, 66−19 — see suspected error note; using the 1-100 chart: 43 + 35 traced as +10 +10 +10 then +1 five times, and shortened to 43 + 30 + 5 = 78; 57 − 42 traced back and shortened to 57 − 40 − 2 = 15; chain loops (+17, +20, +39, then −39, −27, −49); word problem: a train carried 56 adults and 27 children — how many passengers? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 15 |
| Members of an operation — members and result relation: in 47 + 25 = 72 the parts are named addend, addend, sum; in 63 − 28 = 35 — minuend, subtrahend, difference; say which member the red number is in eight equalities; build four equalities from a triple (58, 84, 26 → 58 + 26 = 84, 26 + 58 = 84, 84 − 26 = 58, 84 − 58 = 26); colour the members in boxed equalities; match the + and − signs to the words fewer/increase/decrease/more; compare A 19 + 54, B 35 + 19, C 54 + 35 — which member is 54 in each, compare the sums by reasoning | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 17 |
| Adding and subtracting within 1000: two siblings decided to save money together; the first time the elder put in 700 tögrög and the younger 200 tögrög: a. how many tögrög did they put in in all? (money pictures turned into bar models: the elder's money as 7 bars, the younger's as 2 bars; the expression 700 + 200; how is the sum's value found? 7 hundreds + 2 hundreds = □ hundreds, so 700 + 200 = 900); b. by how many tögrög did the elder put in more than the younger? (expression 700 − 200; to find the money difference compare the bars; 7 hundreds − 2 hundreds = □ hundreds, so 700 − 200 = 500); calculate (2 + 3 / 20 + 30 / 200 + 300; 5 + 3 / 50 + 30 / 500 + 300; 8 − 3 / 80 − 30 / 800 − 300; 9 − 4 / 90 − 40 / 900 − 400) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 98 |
| Three-digit addition without regrouping — Tulga, to buy a book costing 800 tögrög, saved the 200 tögrög he had and saves another 100 tögrög every day — in 5 days can he buy the book?; complete the skipped numbers on the line (120, 220, □, 420, □, 720, □); to buy one sweet (240₮) and one ice cream (730₮), how many tögrög do you pay (expression 240 + 730)?; a chocolate cost 980 tögrög and became 550 tögrög — by how many tögrög did it get cheaper (980 − 550)?; the method: split the numbers into place addends (200 + 40 + 700 + 30 = 900 + 70 = □; 980 − 500 − 50 = 480 − 50 = □); putting the numbers in a place table: for 240 + 730 add hundreds (2з + 7з), tens (4а + 3а), ones (0н + 0н); for 980 − 550 subtract hundreds (9з − 5з), tens (8а − 5а), ones (0н − 0н); evaluate (250 + 230; 710 + 120; 480 + 510; 350 + 610; 110 + 370; 230 + 370; 840 + 150; 760 + 220); evaluate (750 − 230; 710 − 130; 480 − 310; 950 − 610; 670 − 370; 830 − 370; 840 − 450; 760 − 250) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 99 |
| Adding tens and hundreds to three-digit numbers — observe the calculations and discuss (36 + 10 = 46: (30 + 6) + 10 = (30 + 10) + 6 = 40 + 6 = 46; 36 − 10 = 26; 536 + 100 = 636: (500 + 30 + 6) + 100 = (500 + 100) + 30 + 6 = 600 + 30 + 6 = 636; 536 − 100 = 436); the rules in speech: when a ten is added to (subtracted from) a number, its tens place increases (decreases) by 1; when 100 is added (subtracted), understand that the hundreds place increases (decreases) by 1; evaluate the families (41 + 10, 41 + 20, 41 + 30; 41 − 10, 41 − 20, 41 − 30; 627 + 10, 627 + 20, 627 + 30; 627 − 10, 627 − 20, 627 − 30); who calculated correctly on the board — 375 + 200 = ?: Amar: 395; Khulan: 575; Tulga: 595 — who made what mistake?; calculate mentally (142 + 100; 243 − 100; 328 + 200; 510 − 200; 385 + 400; 504 − 400; 163 + 300; 274 − 100); observe each item's price (730₮, 850₮, 570₮, 200₮, 960₮) and answer the questions: to buy each item, how many hundred-tögrög notes are needed? how many ten-tögrög notes? how many one-tögrög notes?; if the tens-place digit of each item's price is swapped, by how many tögrög does each item's price change?; if the hundreds-place digit is swapped with 4, by how many tögrög does each item's price change? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 100 |
| Column addition with regrouping in tens — pupils took part in cleaning along the river bank: before noon they collected 67 bottles and after noon 52 bottles — how many bottles in all? (bar model; expression 67 + 52; the sum's value is found like this: the place-table picture and the column form — 1. add the ones: 7 ones + 2 ones = 9 ones; 2. add the tens: 6 tens + 5 tens = 11 tens = 1 hundred 1 ten; 67 + 52 = 119; also by splitting: 67 is 60 and 7, 52 is 50 and 2, 110 and 9 join to make 119; answer: 1 hundred 1 ten and 9 ones make 119); do the additions (59 + 42; 86 + 53; 56 + 48; 95 + 35; 76 + 65; 68 + 49; 84 + 37; 74 + 41); write expressions from the statements and find their values (increase 65 by the product of 4 and 7; increase 57 by the quotient of 48 and 8; increase the product of 4 and 8 by 93); tourists travelled 75 kilometres on the first day and 58 kilometres the next day — how many kilometres did the tourists travel in all? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 101 |
| Three-digit practice and multi-step problems — evaluate (372 + 120; 426 + 230; 513 + 160; 205 + 560; 714 + 35; 428 + 51; 653 + 245; 426 + 312); evaluate (468 − 140; 719 − 610; 846 − 510; 790 − 280; 894 − 32; 547 − 44; 729 − 316; 477 − 153); write expressions from the statements and find their values (increase the sum of 216 and 361 by 200; decrease the sum of 527 and 452 by 350; increase the difference of 846 and 514 by 217; decrease the difference of 675 and 243 by 421); each of 4 sheets has 121 flowers printed on it and 10 flowers were erased from each sheet — how many flowers are left on the 4 sheets in all?; compose problems matching the expressions (400 + 231 − 510; 314 + 120 + 461; 647 − 321 + 453) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 104 |
| Consolidation (Бататгах): observe the pattern and write the next 3 numbers (10, 25, 40, □, □, □; 16, 40, 64, □, □, □; 91, 75, 59, □, □, □); write the expressions and find their values (decrease the sum of 245 and 314 by 438; increase the difference of 412 and 125 by 211; increase the difference of 768 and 325 by 526; decrease the difference of 987 and 621 by 145); when weighed on a stringless balance, 1 notebook balances 5 pens — then 8 notebooks balance how many pens' weight?; when bundling in threes the remainder is 2 — how many such numbers less than 20 are there? Test it; place the numbers up to 9 in the circles drawn on the sides of a triangle so that the sum of the numbers written on each side becomes 20 — can it be done (a triangle of 9 circles)?; draw the two-row table in your notebook and complete it (22 with the snake pairs 1 and 21, 20 and 2, 3 and 19, …, 17 and …); did you name the picture from the symmetric pictures (a butterfly, a car, a dolphin, a teddy bear, a fox)?; Bulgan picked 18 kg of berries: Tüdev picked 2 times more bilberries (нэрс) than Bulgan, and Sanchir picked 2 times fewer strawberries than Bulgan — find the difference between the berries Tüdev and Sanchir picked | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 128 |
| Consolidation: easy sums, durations, self-check — calculate the sums' values by an easy method (29 + 41 + 59 + 71; 99 + 85 + 101 + 15 + 100; 151 + 19 + 49 + 81 + 50); draw the table in your notebook and find the matching number (a △ row and a □ row with column sums 102, 71, 98, 96, 100); Naran's father gave Naran 26 sweets and gave the younger sibling 13 sweets — how many sweets did Naran's father have in all?; calculate the continuing durations (four pairs of clock faces, start and end); a self-check: 1. find the biggest number divisible by 6 among the following (16; 24; 86; 84 as printed); 2. among the following numbers find a number which, divided by 5, gives remainder 1 and is divisible by 3 (15; 34; 46 as printed); 3. when the product of 16 and 5 is decreased 4-fold, how much is it? (25; 30; 20; 17 as printed); 4. when the quotient of 60 and 4 is increased 2-fold, how much is it? (40; 15; 30; 20 as printed) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 132 |
| Consolidation: sets and price changes — find the expressions' values (two-digit additions and subtractions in rows: 46 + 27; 54 + 37; 63 + 29; 17 + 65; 33 + 48; 83 − 56; 72 − 27; 91 − 43; 57 − 48; 64 − 35; three-digit: 233 + 156; 457 + 511; 608 + 261; 384 + 515; 714 + 173; 756 − 243; 593 − 271; 859 − 568; 647 − 346; 839 − 216); write the number that fits the box (12 × 6 < □ < 99 − 19; 125 + 652 < □ < 900 − 120; 25 × 2 < □ < 11 × 2 as printed — the third has no solution, see suspected-error note); bundle the flowers into baskets in equal numbers (14 flowers, 4 baskets pictured) — how many flowers go into one basket?; Tulga has 16 notebooks and 4 times fewer pens than that — how many school supplies does he have in all?; the shop changed the goods' prices as follows — calculate how they changed from the first price (the milk's price first decreased by 300 tögrög, then increased by 200 tögrög; the bread's price first increased by 50 tögrög, then by 10 tögrög; the biscuit's price first increased by 30 tögrög, then decreased by 50 tögrög; the ice cream's price first increased by 30 tögrög, then decreased by 20 tögrög), with price tags pictured (850₮, 820₮, 900₮, 450₮, 600₮) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 134 |
| Consolidation: sets, tables, unit prices — find the expressions' values (126 + 63; 350 + 45; 257 − 25; 758 + 41; 647 − 33; 256 + 123; 747 − 326; 861 + 107; 598 − 235; 654 − 231); complete the missing numbers in the table (a two-row number table from 91 to 110 with blanks) — how many digits were written in all when you wrote them?; in the picture a segment AB of 1 dm length is shown divided into 10 equal parts: mark the 1/2 part of segment AB and express that part's length in centimetres; Naran goes from home to school in 90 minutes, and from school to home in 1 hour 30 minutes — which way does she spend the longer time?; a. observe the table and find each one's price (5 pens pictured against 400₮ in notes and coins; 2 books against 200₮); b. connect the pairs of numbers whose sum is 100 (20, 65, 56, 43, 81 with 19, 44, 80, 35, 57); c. connect the pairs of numbers whose sum is 1000 (800, 450, 100, 370, 500 with 550, 630, 900, 200, 530); count how many triangles and how many quadrilaterals there are in each pictured subdivided rectangle | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 142 |
| Consolidation: sets, trains, digit games — find the expressions' values (87 − 29; 91 + 19; 84 − 78; 69 − 29; 67 + 39; 121 − 19; 342 + 118; 187 + 98; 365 + 116; 963 − 486); calculate mentally (9 × 10; 8 × 7; 9 × 6; 5 × 4; 7 × 6); Margad planted 19 trees and Dölgöön planted 2 times more trees than Margad — how many trees did they plant in all together?; find the total number of legs of the pictured animals (7 horses and 6 chickens); in how many ways can a three-digit number be composed using the given 3 digits — test using cards (a. 1, 9, 8; b. 0, 3, 5); write the numbers of passengers on the trains (wagons numbered with a pattern: 162, 182, 202, then wagons 4, 5, 6 unknown): observe the numbers' pattern and find how many passengers are on each of trains 4, 5, 6; if 40 passengers got off train 2 and 30 more passengers boarded train 1, does the trains' passenger count change — why?; train 1's passenger count is how many fewer than train 5's passenger count? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 148 |
| Consolidation: brackets, plans, eggs — find the expressions' values (91 − 7 × 6; 82 + 9 × 2; 36 + 8 × 8; 10 × 7 − 12; 5 × 8 + 6 × 10; 8 × 3 + 46); observe the pattern and complete the tables (a 3 × 3 grid with 1, 5, 3 and 1; a shape grid); grandfather fed the chickens: today 47 chickens laid eggs — he packed the eggs 6 equally into baskets: how many eggs did he pack in each basket, and are there eggs left unpacked?; Saruul showed her plan in pictures (a table of start time, end time and activity: singing club, lessons, playing with friends): a. how many minutes did Saruul's singing club last? b. how many hours did she sit at lessons? c. how many minutes did she spend with her friends? d. how many hours of activity did she plan in all?; find the expressions' values (547 − (86 + 29) − 402; 230 + (51 − 32) − 138; 600 − 5 × 8 + 40; 63 ÷ 9 × 7 + 230); write the expressions and find their values (decrease the sum of 16 and 79 by 36; decrease the sum of 69 and 96 by 81; increase the product of 9 and 10 by 96) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 150 |
| Revision: regrouping, finding planted errors — worked example 3: find the total paid for a 580₮ brush and a 350₮ ruler and explain the calculation: 580 + 350 = 500 + 80 + 300 + 50 = 800 + 130 = 800 + 100 + 30 = 900 + 30 = 930; in the place table and column steps: first add the ones (0 + 0 = 0); then the tens: 8 tens + 5 tens = 13 tens, 13 tens = 1 hundred 3 tens — remember 1 hundred; then the hundreds: 5 hundreds + 3 hundreds = 8 hundreds, 8 hundreds + 1 hundred = 9 hundreds; find which of the column additions contain errors and explain them (258 + 3 written as 251; 258 + 63 as 311; 258 + 463 as 621; 547 + 286 as 733 — all four are planted wrong answers; correct values 261, 321, 721, 833); find the sums' values (63 + 25; 923 + 9; 320 + 680; 238 + 641; 400 + 500; 350 + 150; 7 + 116; 503 + 270); find the perimeter of a gauze frame (жааз) 29 cm long and 18 cm wide; explain how the addition is done using a number line: 323 + 49 = ? (jumps: 323 to 330, then tens, then 2, ending at 372); name the given shapes: how many sides and how many vertices has each shape; is there a shape whose name you do not know (a pentagon, a rhombus, a triangle, a hexagon, a circle)? Discuss how the shapes differ | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 7 |
| Addition properties: commutative, zero, associative — adding and subtracting within ten thousand: there were red and green apples on a plate; two pupils found the total number of apples using cubes: Pupil 1 wrote 3 + 5, Pupil 2 wrote 5 + 3 (3 + 5 □ 5 + 3): complete the conclusion — "when the addends' places are swapped, the sum's value …"; two pupils played a ring-toss game and their totals came out 3 + 0 and 0 + 3: complete the sentences — "when zero is added to a number …"; "when a number is added to zero …"; there were beads of three colours: by how many methods can the total number of beads be found (4 + 5 + 6: Pupil 1 grouped (4 + 5) + 6, Pupil 2 grouped 4 + (5 + 6), Pupil 3 grouped (4 + 6) + 5, all giving 15); worked example 1: explain the calculations (198 + 54 = 198 + (2 + 52) = (198 + 2) + 52 = 200 + 52 = 252; 86 + 67 = 86 + (14 + 53) = (86 + 14) + 53 = 100 + 53 = 153 — completing the first addend to a round number); find the values of the expressions (3 + 8 + 7; 30 + 80 + 70; 300 + 180 + 20; 240 + 90 + 60; 135 + 5 + 60; 192 + 8 + 38; 58 + 3 + 27; 96 + 48; 236 + 235 + 4; 195 + 65; 87 + 73); a boy saved 350, 180 and 150 tögrög — how many tögrög did he save in all? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 23 |
| Column addition within 10,000 — a notebook costs 750₮ and an eraser 230₮ — when buying one of each, how many tögrög are paid (the expression 750 + 230)? A book costs 3750₮ and plain pencils 1230₮ — when buying one of each, how much is paid (the expression 3750 + 1230)?; calculate by splitting the numbers into place addends (700 + 50 + 200 + 30 = 700 + 200 + 50 + 30 = 900 + 80 = 980; 3000 + 1000 + 700 + … + … + … = 4000 + … + … = …); place the numbers in a place table and then in columns: 750 + 230 (7 hundreds + 2 hundreds, 5 tens + 3 tens, 0 + 0 ones → 980; answer: 980 tögrög are paid) and 3750 + 1230 (3 thousand + 1 thousand, 7 + 2 hundreds, 5 + 3 tens, 0 + 0 → 4980; answer: 4980 tögrög); calculate (2750 + 230; 750 + 4230; 1750 + 5230; 3545 + 2314); find the sums' values following the template and complete the column writing (4569 + 2373 built step by step with the carried 1s marked: 4569 + 2373 = 6942; a speech asks: adding a three-digit number is just the same as with the numbers you know — how would you check whether the sum's value is right?); do the operations (3538 + 2365; 3665 + 4189; 1951 + 5930; 3508 + 4192; 1585 + 5256; 2097 + 3904; 2358 + 4342; 4259 + 651; 5605 + 2395; 6741 + 3157; 5163 + 3647; 1826 + 7184) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 25 |
| Estimating sums and prices — worked example 3: calculate approximately: a shop sold 343 pairs of shoes in the first month and 435 pairs in the second month: find approximately how many pairs of shoes were sold in the two months: round the numbers by hundreds: 343 becomes 300, 435 becomes 400, 300 + 400 = 700 — so about 700 pairs of shoes were sold; round the numbers by tens: 343 becomes 340, 435 becomes 440, 340 + 440 = 780 — so about 780 pairs; afterwards add exactly: 343 + 435 = (300 + 40 + 3) + (400 + 30 + 5) = 700 + 70 + 8 = 778 pairs; find the total paid, approximately: a. a 4500₮ bowl, a 2700₮ spoon and a ladle (?); b. a 5850₮ shirt and a 1990₮ toothbrush set; c. a 1750₮ item, a 2490₮ juice and 2790₮ raisins — then calculate exactly; when buying the following goods for 10000 tögrög, how many tögrög of change will be taken (1 kg of bananas 4990₮; 1 kg of apples 5490₮)?; paints and a brush are priced (watercolour paint 3750₮; gouache 2560₮; a brush 730₮): the goods were bought by paying with a banknote — how many tögrög of change are taken?; by approximately how many tögrög did each good become cheaper (price tables, before → after: a ball pen 800₮ → 590₮; a notebook 250₮ → 190₮; oil pencils 4000₮ → 2990₮; a plain pencil 1200₮ → 790₮; plain paper 2300₮ → 1690₮; 5 pencil cases 6420₮ → 5630₮; toothpaste 2170₮ → 1840₮; a spoon-and-fork set 5500₮ → 4900₮); by how much did each become cheaper, and what is the difference between the exact difference and the approximate difference? Which goods can be bought within (багтаан) 10000 tögrög?; self-check: calculate (165 + 5337; 2136 + 3864; 8810 − 5879; 9540 − 5647); Khaliun bought watercolour paint for 2850₮ and a drawing notebook for 1260₮ — how many tögrög did she pay in all? Find the price difference between the watercolour paint and the drawing notebook | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 28 |
| Consolidation: adding/subtracting within 10,000 — adding and subtracting numbers within ten thousand: a loaded lorry got stuck at a bridge marked 10 t: an empty car weighs 4250 kg, and loaded it weighs 5345 kg more: can the loaded car cross the bridge? (the working is shown: 4250 + 5345 = 9595; 1 t = 1000 kg; 10 t = 10000 kg; 9595 kg < 10000 kg: the car can cross the bridge) — observe the calculation and discuss it; Bayar had 11 apples: after eating some of them, 8 were left: which equality expresses this action (a. 11 + 8 = □; b. 8 = 11 + □; c. 11 − □ = 8; d. 8 − □ = 11)?; answer the questions: a. one of two sacks has 5 kg and the other 1 kg of vegetables: how can the load carried in the two hands be balanced?; b. 50 g and 100 g weights are placed on a balance's two pans: the weights on one pan total 700 g and on the other pan 800 g: how can the balance be brought level (move the 50 g weight to the other pan)?; the prices of stationery goods were cheapened: fill in the missing numbers in the table of prices and say what these numbers express (a bag: first price 32500₮, price after cheapening 28300₮, the difference □; a bundle of writing paper: 6800₮, □, difference 1200₮; a thick notebook: □, 1560₮, difference 450₮; water paints: 4500₮, 3300₮, difference □): if a bag and a thick notebook are bought, how many tögrög are saved?; find the numbers such that the sums of the three numbers lying in a column, a row and a diagonal direction of the table are equal (three 3 × 3 grids: one holding 309, 319, 311 and 313; another holding 113, 119, 125 and 121; a third empty); find the expressions' values (7523 + 2157; 6301 + 3849; 9456 − 6508; 6809 − 2953); the teacher set the task of finding the sum of two numbers and checked them: Chimgee and Tulgaa checked in different ways: how did each check (the worked addition 4507 + 3195 = 7702 is checked by Chimgee by subtracting one addend and by Tulgaa by subtracting the other addend)?; mountain problems: a. climbers of the "Khuiten" peak of the Tavanbogd mountain in the Altai range sent word by phone: "We are at a height of 3795 m. 579 m remains to the peak": find the height of Mongolia's highest point, the Khuiten peak (4374 m); b. another peak, Ottontenger, is raised 4008 m above sea level: the Khuiten peak is how many metres higher than Ottontenger (366 m)?; c. the world's highest peak, Everest, is 8848 m above sea level: compare the world's highest point with Mongolia's highest point: what is their difference (4474 m)?; choose a suitable method and calculate (375 + 199; 7540 + 1960; 2786 − 995; 6942 − 1850); a self-check: 1) find the values of the sums and differences (2520 + 1107; 301 + 3240; 9006 − 6500); 2) a construction company brought in sand by lorry: the lorry's front part carried 5250 kg and its trailer 4350 kg of sand: how many kilograms of sand did the lorry carry in all (9600 kg)?; 3) a traveller went from Ulaanbaatar to Kharkhorin and from there on to Khorgo: using the distances given on the map diagram (Ulaanbaatar–Kharkhorin and Kharkhorin–Khorgo), find the length of the road travelled and which way to Khorgo is shorter | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 129–130 |
| Consolidation word problems — a garment factory has 2 departments: the first department has 245 seamstresses and the second 140: when the factory's organisation changed, the first department's seamstresses decreased by 50 and the second department's increased by 53: how did the factory's total number of seamstresses change (it grew by 3)?; observe the table: write the expression that fits the empty cells and check it: explain how you found it (two tables that keep the result constant while the terms change: the addends table — 2530 and 1374 making the sum 3904, with the variants 2530 + 470, 2530 − 530, 1374 + 626 and 1374 − 374; the minuend-and-subtrahend table — 8650 and 2640 making the difference 6010, with equal changes to both terms); c. compose a problem using the table's information; find the pairs of numbers of which one is exactly one hundred bigger than the other (from: 196 and 206; 654 and 1754; 58 and 568; 9230 and 9240; 2100 and 3100; 6750 and 6650); Sarnai bought a fruit juice for 2500₮ and apples for 3750₮ at the shop: Chimgee bought a drink for 2900₮ and nuts for 5600₮: who spent more money, and by how much (Chimgee, by 2250₮)?; our school's three classes have 174 boys and 219 girls: of the pupils, 189 entered school at the age of 7 and the rest at the age of 6: how many children entered school at the age of 6 (204)?; compose a problem from the brief record (There was 410 kg; Sold 90 kg and 160 kg; Left ?); a school has 2035 pupils: of them, 1020 study in art circles and 915 in sports circles: if 108 pupils study in no circle at all, how many pupils study in circles? How many pupils study in both the art and the sports circles (1927 attend a circle; 8 attend both — by inclusion and exclusion)?; three classes collected 950 kg of waste paper in all: the first class collected 427 kg, and the second class collected 192 kg less waste paper than the first class: how many kilograms of waste paper did the third class collect (288 kg)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 131 |
| Consolidation: mixed word problems — Zorigt travelled 609 km by plane, 382 km by car and 29 km by horse: how many kilometres did Zorigt travel in all (1020 km)?; a picture shows how far Bulgan and Erdenet lie from Ulaanbaatar (Ulaanbaatar to Erdenet 370 km, Erdenet to Bulgan 60 km): a. at what distance from Ulaanbaatar does Erdenet lie?; b. if a traveller goes from Bulgan to Ulaanbaatar passing through Erdenet, how many kilometres does he travel in all (430 km)?; c. a walking traveller going towards Bulgan met a person on horseback on the road: how far had the traveller from Bulgan travelled?; d. the walking traveller travels 6 km in an hour: in how many hours will he travel from Erdenet to Bulgan (10 hours)?; find approximately the length of a writing paperclip (two rulers are pictured with paperclips lying along them); there are 23 l of water in a vessel: to empty the vessel, the water was poured into small vessels: a. to pack all the water, how many vessels of 2 litres capacity are needed? Why (12, the last only half full)?; b. how many vessels of 3 litres capacity are needed? Why (8)?; c. there are 2 vessels of 3 litres capacity and 12 vessels of 2 litres capacity: to pack all the water, how many vessels of 2 l and 3 l are needed in total at the fewest? Why (3 × 3 l + 7 × 2 l = 23 l: 10 vessels)?; the tree pictured is 3 m high: find the building's height approximately: explain the method you found; a teacher gave one ribbon to each of four children: each child measured their own ribbon's length: the second child's ribbon is 2 times longer than the first child's, the third child's ribbon is 3 times longer than the first child's, and the fourth child's ribbon is 2 cm longer than the third child's: if the four children's ribbons' lengths total 1 m 10 cm in all (the variant total 2 m 10 cm is also printed), calculate each child's ribbon in centimetres | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 138 |
| Ordering and money puzzles; mental subtraction — Davaa, Bulgan and Tamir played a game: they scored 725, 510 and 255 points: using the clues (Bulgan did not come third, and the order by points names Davaa), arrange the points in order and find who scored what; Khongor has 4432₮: Tuya has 829₮ less than Khongor, and Bat has 1280₮ more than Tuya: after the children bought presents with the money they had, Khongor had 1643₮ left, Tuya 1043₮ and Bat 2999₮: who spent the most money (Khongor: he spent 2789₮)?; a printer prints 10 pages in a minute: in how many hours will it print 3000 pages (5 hours)?; calculate mentally (970 − 799; 871 − 698; 893 − 798; 673 − 496; 770 − 683; 298 − 199; 485 − 389; 772 − 597; 665 − 588; 720 − 504; 890 − 398; 640 − 604 — subtracting by rounding the subtrahend up and compensating); in each picture, find how many different triangles there are (three subdivided triangle figures); an activity: work following the instructions and draw a conclusion: 1. a. choose any two-digit number; b. write the two numbers that follow the number you chose; c. find the product of the biggest and the smallest of the three consecutive numbers; d. multiply the number you chose by itself; e. find the difference between the two products and discuss it; f. repeat the action for other two-digit numbers and observe the differences (the difference is always 1: (n − 1)(n + 1) = n² − 1); 2. a. choose any two-digit number; b. subtract 2 from the chosen number and add 2, and write the three numbers (the example 43, 45, 47); c-e. do the same actions: what is subtracted from the products this time (the difference is always 4)?; 3. from a three-digit number subtract 3 and add 3 to form three numbers, repeat the actions above, and guess and discuss what result comes out (the difference is always 9); a hospital, a holiday camp and a teahouse ordered goods: calculate how many of each thing are needed in all (the hospital: 20 boxes of medicine, 16 bandages, 6 beds; the camp: 16 grounds, 18 tents, 20 blankets; the teahouse: 84 tables, 30 chairs, 42 water vessels); write in decreasing order (52482, 50023, 56028, 63340; 145009, 154032, 145004, 159023; 87035, 80562, 78035, 79003; 138023, 138032, 139006, 183487); write in your notebook and compare (2300 kg □ 2 t 400 kg; 8700 g □ 8 kg 900 g; 275 cm □ 2 m 75 cm; 27 l 500 ml □ 26 l 900 ml) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 12 |
| Adding/subtracting up to a million in columns — adding and subtracting numbers up to a million (Сая хүртэлх тоог нэмэх, хасах): to Mongolia's beautiful places, 367000 tourists came in the first year and 485900 tourists in the following year: how many tourists came in the two years in all? By how much did the following year's number of tourists increase? a. find the total number of tourists (the column addition is laid out in the place-class table: 367000 + 485900 = 852900, with the carries shown); find the difference (the column subtraction 485900 − 367000 = 118900, with the borrowing shown); b. the increase in the number of tourists is 118900; a grid of round numbers (hundred-thousands, ten-thousands, thousands, hundreds, tens and ones) has some entries circled: use them to make the totals; carry out the actions and check them (column calculations on squared paper with some digits missing: 5752 − 1842; 12911 − 10260; 607411 + 249897; 998623 − 771128); a. the sum of 2 numbers is 27900 and one number is 25600: what is the other number (2300)?; b. the difference of 2 numbers is 37960 and one number is 23680: what can the other number be (61640 or 14280 — both cases)?; using the digits 1, 3, 6, 8 and 9 without repeating them, write the biggest and the smallest five-digit numbers: find their sums' and differences' values; an elder brother had 123000₮ in his savings: the first time he added 87800₮ to the savings and took out 147999₮, and the next time he added 56775₮ to the savings: how many tögrög did the brother's savings become (119576₮)?; find the unknown term (an addend, addend, sum table with missing entries to complete, with sums such as 422067, 110009 and 100000) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 13 |
| Stepping-stone game; newspaper and quiz problems — a board game (ГАРАА — start): a grid of stepping stones holds the numbers 1000, 100, 10 and 1 in rows: 1. a boy collects points on the stepping stones as he crosses to the girl: to arrive at the girl with 3287 points, which stepping stones must he cross?; 2. if the boy first had 1322 points, crossing which stepping stones did he arrive at the girl with 4588 points?; 3. the girl loses the points on the stepping stones as she crosses to the boy: the girl first had 8673 points: crossing which stepping stones does she arrive at the boy, and with how many points?; a postman delivered 200000 newspapers to the post office's 3 branches: he delivered 10200 to the first branch and 1600 more than that to the second branch: how many newspapers did he deliver to the third branch (178000)?; pupils held a quiz competition: a team gets 5 points for each correct answer: at the end of the competition the first team had 55 points and the second team had answered 12 questions correctly: by how many points did a team win the competition (the second team, with 60 points, by 5)?; father bought a 50-page picture album: if 6 pictures are placed on each page of the album, how many pictures does the album hold (300)?; 6020 spectators watched a football championship: of them, 3860 were men, the women spectators were 2840 fewer than the men, and the rest were children: how many children watched (1140)?; carry out the actions with measures (2 m 60 cm − 1 m 50 cm; 70 kg 700 g + 2 kg 900 g; and two more measure additions and subtractions); find the values of the expressions with brackets (45327 − 8524 + (37531 − 19748); 78104 + 124813 − (95425 − 87050); 150035 − (17306 + 24948); 96535 − 60083 + 7848); a self-check: express the unknown term through the other terms (a. □ + 276754 = 429861; b. □ − 152065 = 400154; c. 615654 − □ = 392342; d. 136000 + □ = 130654 — see the suspected-errors note: as printed this has no whole-number solution) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 14 |

**Sub lesson map (added 2026-11-08, whole-arc rule):** the
rows above already stocked the size climb; the text now
teaches it as Sub lesson 4, carried by the Indonesia Grade 5
row (column addition to 100,000), the Ethiopia Grade 4 rows
(adding to 1,000,000; teacher-guide worked example
631,286 + 331,989 = 963,275), the North Macedonia Grade 5
rows (worked 47,586 + 28,342 = 75,928 with regrouping) and
the Rwanda Primary 6 row (column addition beyond 1,000,000
with carrying across periods). No existing row changed.

**Acceptance count: taught in 69 countries (Egypt, Jordan, Libya, Kazakhstan, Greece, Armenia, Vietnam, Mauritania, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, South Africa, Sierra Leone, El Salvador, Cyprus, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Azerbaijan, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Peru, Serbia, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Sweden, Timor-Leste, Turkmenistan).**. Egypt carries addition from its beginnings in Primary 1 (Japanese-collaboration series) — what makes 5 to 10, addition as putting together and as increase, addition stories, adding three numbers — through making 10 and two-digit work in Term 2, and the column algorithm whole in Primary 2 — vertical addition in the new series, regrouping ones and adding more than two numbers in the old Discover book, carrying to hundreds, three-digit columns — with 3- and 4-digit addition in Primary 3 (the Primary 1–3 rows were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep). Jordan carries make-ten and doubles in Grade 1 and regrouping in Grade 2. Libya carries adding without regrouping to 100 in Grade 1, regrouping to 1000 in Grade 2, and to 10,000 in Grade 3. Kazakhstan carries the written column algorithm on named cases in Grade 2. Greece carries vertical addition with carrying in Grade 1 Teuxos B. Armenia carries adding with place transitions in Grade 2. Vietnam carries adding without regrouping in Grade 1 and with regrouping in Grade 2. Mauritania carries addition with carrying in Grade 1. Other read countries teach adding inside their own Grade 1–3 books and join by merge.** Laos carries addition from joining groups within 10 and crossing 10 by making 10 in Grade 1, through 2-digit regrouping and 3-digit place-value addition in Grade 2, to whole-number and decimal addition in Grade 5. Yemen carries addition from joining within 10 in Grade 1 through carrying within 1000, 10,000 and 999,999, with estimating and checking by rounding, to hundreds of millions in Grade 5. Indonesia carries addition from joining to 10 and to 20 in Grade 1, through addition under 20 in Grade 2, column addition to 100 with carrying in Grade 3 and to 1,000 in Grade 4, to column addition to 100,000 in Grade 5, then integer, fraction and decimal addition in Grade 7 (Merdeka and K13 Semester 1). Nicaragua carries addition from number bonds and sums to 10 in Grade 1, through carrying to 100 in Grade 2 and to 1,000 in Grade 3, to a 4–5-digit worked box in Grade 4 (its only addition teaching there), and signed-number addition in Grade 7. Ethiopia carries addition from adding to 10 with commutativity, counting on, making 10 and doubles in Grade 1, through adding to 1000 without regrouping and with carrying in Grade 2, to adding to 10,000 in Grade 3 and to 1,000,000 with commutativity, associativity and identity in Grade 4. South Sudan carries addition from two-digit sums, column addition and missing addends in Grade 1, through number-line addition and column addition with carrying in Grade 2 (26 + 37 = 63; 384 + 208 = 592), to addition to 4 digits in Grade 3 (4321 + 3178 = 7499), and addition of fractions in Secondary 1. One book slip is never taught as fact: the Grade 2 book's p. 17 step "1+8+0=0" in the 384 + 208 worked example is wrong (it should be 9; the final answer 592 is right) — teach the correct step. Iraq carries addition from to 10 and to 20 in Grade 1 — no regrouping, making 10 — through regrouping to 999 in Grade 2 and to 9999 in Grade 3, to addition within millions in Grade 4, and to adding large numbers within billions in Grade 5. Bangladesh carries addition from the concept within 10 with the making-10 strategy in Class 1, through addition with and without carry to 3 digits in Class 2 and to 5 digits with carry in Class 3, to 5-digit addition in Class 4, and addition on the number line and of integers in Class 6. Nepal carries addition from bonds to 5, 9, 10 and 19 and two-digit addition without carrying in Grade 1, through 2- and 3-digit addition with carrying in Grade 2 (both books) and addition to 4 digits with carrying in Grade 3, to addition of fractions and decimals in Grades 5–6. Cambodia carries addition whole from single digits in Grade 1 through decimals, fractions and rationals in Grades 4–8, with the properties in Grade 7. Kuwait carries addition up its whole ladder — to 10 in Grade 1, within 100 in Grades 1–2, 3-digit numbers in Grade 2, to 10,000 in Grade 3, to millions in Grade 4, whole numbers and decimals in Grade 5, integers in Grade 6 and rational numbers in Grade 8. North Macedonia carries addition at Grade 1 (2022) — sums to 5, to 10 and to 20, number bonds, commutativity met as adding in a different order, and the make-ten strategy for three numbers. Iran carries addition from the first written expressions in Grade 1 (§13) through carrying in Grade 2 to larger numbers in Grade 3, and keeps it in use through the primary years.

Ghana also contributes to this lesson as curriculum, teacher and student-workbook (task-sheet) evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook — and Ghana is not counted in the taught-in figure above. (Ghana rows stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.)

China carries addition from Grade 1 carrying work to the Grade 4 treatment of the operation's part-relations and its laws stated in letters.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries addition from the Primer's “one more” to 9-digit column addition in Class 5 and integer addition in Class 6.

Syria builds addition slowly and explicitly — number bonds for every number to 12 before the + sign appears, completing-to-ten in Grade 1 Semester 2, carrying introduced in Grade 2 as ringing ten ones and exchanging them for a ten-rod, columns to four digits in Grade 3 and five in Grade 4, a restatement with the commutative law in Grades 5–6, and rational-number addition in Grade 7.

Saudi Arabia builds addition from part–whole stories and number bonds in Grade 1, through counting on, doubles and making ten in Grade 2 Part One, to regrouped three-digit addition in Part Two, the three properties and estimation-checked answers in Grade 3, and mental grouping with five-digit columns in Grade 4, with the older 1445 Term-3 volume witnessing the Grade 2 Part Two chapter.

Rwanda teaches addition as a staged climb — to 200, 500 and 999 in Year 2, to 2,000, 5,000 and 10,000 in Year 3 with one-step word problems in a given / request / steps-and-solution format, column addition in P4, column addition of 6/7-digit numbers in P5, addition beyond 1,000,000 in P6, and addition of natural numbers and of integers, with its properties, in Senior 1.

Sudan builds addition inside 9 in Grade 1 — picture addition, number bonds, vertical addition, counting on and adding three numbers — then two-digit addition without carrying, addition within 999 in Grade 2 and within 9999 in Grade 3.

India carries addition through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 1–5 and Mathematics Class 6–7 — including one more; adding on a number snake; adding 10 on a 100-grid; adding distances in km.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик I, II, III, IV, V (Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5): addition built as joining and counting on from the first number bonds, through column addition to a million, with the mixed consolidation sets at the end of each primary book exercising addition and subtraction together.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contemos... 1, 2, 3 y 4* (Grade 1), *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2) and *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3). From its books: from Grade 1: addition as joining — joining two collections of leaves: 4 + 3 = 7 and 6 + 4 = 10, read in words and symbols; from Grade 2: adding without carrying — mental addition of small collections (crayons 1+1+1+5+10+10 = 28); sums that stay within the ten; from Grade 3: adding large numbers — ellipsis notation (1 + 2 + 3 + … + 100); Gauss's pairing gives 5.050 for 1 to 100, extended to 1–200 and 1–1.000 as exercises.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 1: addition as joining like objects; the + and = signs (Lesson 12); addition written vertically (column form); addition in both horizontal and vertical forms (Lesson 13); swapping the addends does not change the sum (Lesson 14); number bonds to 2, 3, 4 and 5 (number houses); missing addends; number bonds to 8: missing addends and column work; number bonds to 9: missing addends and column work; mixed addition and subtraction drill (worked 3+2=5); the value of zero in addition: n + 0 and 0 + n (Lesson 17); mixed addition and subtraction with missing numbers; circle the correct sum (worked examples); mixed addition and subtraction drill to 9; addition and subtraction from pictures (Lesson 19); addition practice: paired sums, column answers given; addition tables and the addition staircase (1+1 up to 4+5); adding 10 + a one-digit number (Lesson 22); 10 + one-digit number from pictures: write the sum sentence; adding a two-digit number and a one-digit number: column practice within 20; adding one-digit numbers with one- and two-digit numbers: horizontal practice; adding complete tens: 30 + 20 = 50, 30 + 40 = 70 (Lesson 37); adding complete tens with ten-frame pictures: 50 + 30 = 80; adding a two-digit number and a one-digit number, no carrying (Lesson 38); two-digit + one-digit practice, horizontal and column; adding two two-digit numbers with ones/tens tables, no carrying (Lesson 39); adding two two-digit numbers horizontally and vertically (Lesson 40); adding two two-digit numbers: column practice with place headings; mixed two-digit addition and subtraction: column practice; mixed two-digit addition and subtraction: horizontal practice; word problems: addition stories (Lesson 43, problems 1-6, 8, 9); from Grade 4: review: addition in vertical and horizontal form (Lesson 3); addition of numbers to a milliard (Lesson 9); checking an addition (Lesson 10). The Grade 1 Dari edition's own differing exercise items are stocked as Dari-edition rows above.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 1 (teacher's book): Components of numbers: addition as combining two sets and as counting on; conservation of number; picture 6 +…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: understand addition as counting on and combining sets; know components of 6, 7, 8, 9…; Components for 6–9: name 6, 7, 8, 9 as two other numbers, use = sign; bonds for 6 (6−1=5, 5+1=6, 4+2=6,…; Components for 10: bonds for 10 with bead strings and coins; 9+1=10, 1+9=10, complements 9→1, 6→4, example…; Components of 6, 7, 8, 9, 10 and commutativity of addition; ways to make 4 printed as 0+4, 1+3, 2+2, 1+3, 4+0…; Addition: addition as counting on one by one and as grouping; a + b = c with parts and whole; commutativity…; Chapter opener, Diskuto: count on and group; find a total by pairing addends that make 10, e.g. 4 + 3 + 6 =…; Addition by grouping: understand addition as grouping two sets, write number equations; splits of 10 as…; Sums to 20 / Adding by counting: add one by one counting on; 3 and 5 make 8, 5 + 2 = 7 in another colour…; Unë tani di: revision with a +2 then +6 number machine (4 + 2 = 6 and 6 + 6 = 12) and a board game of…; Near doubles: find near doubles using known doubles, e.g. 5 + 6; double of 4 is 8 and its near doubles are 7…. from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Addition and subtraction together; addition to 1 000; column addition to 10 000. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Compensation strategy (plans 17–18); Opener + 4A: addition and subtraction of 2- and 3-digit numbers (plans 27–29); Opener + 3A: mental addition and subtraction strategies (plans 35–36).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Adición y sustracción de enteros; Operaciones combinadas de suma y resta; from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Los números enteros (Z).

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Addition of natural numbers (addends, sum), shown on the coordinate ray; Addition of multi-digit numbers in columns via place-value expansion.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 1 Part One: components (number bonds) of numbers to 5: all two-part splits, incl. components of 4 in full; components of 6 and 7 by splitting cube towers and sets; components of 9 (practical activity); equal and consecutive components; addition within 9 as combining two pictured groups; horizontal addition sentences; missing addend within 9; form an addition sentence from two chosen numbers; word problem: how many more needed (5 balloons, needs 9); word problem: two girls' olive baskets combined (3 + 2); from Grade 1 Part Two: addition within 10 consolidated: choose two boxes totalling 7 or 5; all splits of 10; represent addition on the number line and find the result (within 10); addition within 18 from number components; pocket-money splits of 9 qirsh; doubles: split a set into two equal components and write the addition sentence; near-doubles strategy: 6 + 7 = (6 + 6) + 1, 8 + 9, 7 + 8; missing addends (7 + □ = 15); word problem: 8 morning + 9 evening Eid visitors = 17; addition within 18 (II): add 10 + a number (spinner game); make-ten strategy: enclose ten first, then add the rest; make-ten by moving counters to complete the first set to ten, then add; complete the larger addend to ten in steps: 8 + 3 = 8 + 2 + 1 = 10 + 1; two methods to one sum: make-ten versus equal-components (doubles) routes compared in a table; unit review: mental comparison of sums with signs; 18 pupils, equal boys and girls — how many girls.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its syllabi require addition of whole numbers at every class — up to 999 at Class 1 (with addition facts to 18 recalled), four-digit numbers at Class 2, up to 99,999 at Class 3 and up to 9,999,999 at Class 4, with and without regrouping. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 3: crossword of operation vocabulary: + and − signs, difference (fark), minuend (eksilen), subtrahend (çıkan), sum (toplam), addend (toplanan), plus place-value clues (935's tens value 30; 806's hundreds digit 8) and 18 + 21; sort story situations: colour addition situations green, subtraction situations red (library 245 + 138 books; money box 963 − 485 spent; bakery 375 + 248 loaves; 728 pupils, 194 on a trip); column addition without carrying, terms named: 152 + 135 = 287 with addend, addend, sum labelled and the same sum built in base-ten blocks; add ones, then tens, then hundreds; swapping the addends does not change the sum: 132 + 124 and 124 + 132 for two days' bread sales; then addition with carrying introduced: 148 + 264 = 412 in place-value words; carrying step by step, step 1 and 2: ones make 12 ones = 1 ten + 2 ones carried to the tens; tens make 11 tens = 1 hundred + 1 ten carried to the hundreds (148 + 264); carrying step 3: the hundreds are added with the carried hundred, total 412; say what to do when there is a carry from the ones and from the tens; add three three-digit numbers in columns with two carries: a fishmonger's weekend sales 197 + 232 + 323 = 752 kg, also shown in base-ten blocks; mental addition game: bowling with numbered bottle caps (100, 350, 321, 198, 200, 250, 168, 123) — knock down two bottles and add their numbers in your head; mental addition by splitting into place values: 132 + 243 = (100+200) + (30+40) + (2+3) = 375; and by splitting one addend onto the other: 186 + 13 = 186 + 10 + 3 = 199; analyse an addition by place values in a food table: egg 53 g + walnut 25 g = 78 g done in columns and split as (50 + 20) + (3 + 5); model a chosen addition in base-ten blocks; three-digit addition restated on a library sum: 232 + 127 = 359 in blocks and columns; start at the ones as with two-digit numbers, watch the carries, step by step; calculate and match results (525 + 303, 684 − 433, 659 + 280, 540 − 213 against 251, 939, 347, 327, 828 — one result is a spare) and find missing numbers in operations (365 + … = 548; 657 − … = 458; … + 175 = 530; … − 248 = 609); from Grade 7: Integer addition modelled as moves right (+) and left (−) on the number line (game scores); Integer addition rule: same signs — add absolute values and keep the common sign; different signs — subtract the smaller absolute value from the larger and take the sign of the larger.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: addend and sum, the words of addition; horizontal and vertical addition without regrouping to 999,999; addition with regrouping step by step by place (one intermediate-total misprint taught corrected); adding three whole numbers; word problems of herds and lorry loads.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 1, Part 1 (student textbook-notebook, 2025); Mathematics 3, Part 1 (student); the Mathematics 1 methodological guide (teacher evidence); Mathematics 2, Part I (student); Mathematics 5 (student): addition (first introduction, on objects); addition (continued); adding and subtracting multi-digit numbers; addition within 10; addition and subtraction within 20; adding and subtracting tens; adding two-digit numbers; column addition of multi-digit numbers.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 42 teacher-evidence items from 20 of the guides (Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4, Book 5, Book 8, Book 9, Book 10, Book 11, Book 12, Book 13, Book 15, Book 19, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30, Book 31, Book 32; Grades 1, 2, 3, 4, 5).

**Book 1:** *Le Monde des Maths 1, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 1re année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 1,775,505 bytes, 201 PDF pages. The collection follows the revised curriculum/programmes of July 2020 (32 lessons in 6 units over 4 domains); items are the guide's 32 numbered lessons, printed pp. from its Table des matières. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. Same collection and July-2020 revised programme as Book 1; items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where the sommaire prints them, later lessons cited by sequence (seq.). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 3:** *L'Explicite en Maths CP, Guide pédagogique* (CP = Grade 1) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,092,403 bytes, 116 PDF pages. The explicit-teaching method guide for the pioneer-schools programme; its Chapter 3 gives worked lesson sheets for the SAME 32-lesson Grade 1 programme as Book 1 — a shared programme, two different guides, cited separately, never merged. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 4:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 1, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 7,559,907 bytes; new edition September 2019, with ministry approval panel. Its contents table lists the same 32-lesson revised programme as Books 1 and 3, in Arabic; the PDF's legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so the contents were read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 5:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE1, Guide pédagogique* (CE1 = Grade 2) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,580,797 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 2 programme corresponding to Book 2. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 8:** *Switch On! 1re année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,979,650 bytes, 88 PDF pages. A 5-minute daily calculation-drill guide (Japanese method, dir. Prof. Akira Sato); items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire (tracks A and B, weeks 1–34). Its graphisme weeks 1–2 (pencil-stroke practice, no mathematics content) and its confirmation tests (assessment apparatus) are excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 9:** *Switch On! Every Day CP, livret d'activités rituelles* (Grade 1, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,665,582 bytes, 109 PDF pages. The pupil activity booklet WITH the answers worked, filed by the publisher in its teacher-guide library and staged as a teacher edition — teacher evidence of what the pupil booklet practises, not a pupil textbook in hand. Its avant-propos states the design: daily 10-minute rituals — pages A/C mental calculation, B/D written calculation, page E varied fundamental concepts (measures, geometry, data handling). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 10:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 3, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 18,309,585 bytes, 351 PDF pages. Its contents (read from rendered pages — legacy font encoding) list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, the Grade 3 programme. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 11:** *Switch On! 2e année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,214,244 bytes, 88 PDF pages. Items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire (weeks 1–34); the confirmation tests are excluded in the placements as assessment apparatus. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 12:** *Switch On! 3e année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 3, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 3,536,222 bytes, 88 PDF pages. Items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire; confirmation tests excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 13:** *Switch On! 4e année, Guide de l'enseignant* (teacher guide, Grade 4, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 5,239,501 bytes, 88 PDF pages. Items are the distinct drill strands in its Sommaire; confirmation tests excluded in the placements. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 15:** *Switch On! Every Day CE1, livret d'activités rituelles* (Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 6,449,715 bytes, 109 PDF pages. Same series and standing as Book 9: the pupil booklet with answers, filed as a teacher edition — the A/C mental, B/D written, E varied-concepts daily design. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 19:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 5, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 70,552,470 bytes, 330 PDF pages; new edition September 2020, ministry approval serial 515220 (3 Aug 2020). Its contents list 32 numbered lessons in 6 units, read from rendered pages (legacy font encoding). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 26:** *Le Monde des Maths 4, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 4e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 4, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 4,953,260 bytes, 190 PDF pages; read in place from its staging copy (never deleted from staging). Items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where printed, later units by sequence. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 27:** *Le Monde des Maths 3, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 3e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 3, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 6,204,899 bytes. Items are its 32 numbered lessons — printed pp. from its Table des matières where printed, later units by sequence; the Grade 3 programme, corresponding to Book 10. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 28:** *L'Explicite en Maths CE2, Guide pédagogique* (CE2 = Grade 3) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,578,274 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 3 programme, corresponding to Books 10 and 27. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 29:** *L'Explicite en Maths CM1, Guide pédagogique* (CM1 = Grade 4) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 8,896,704 bytes. Items are its 32 lesson sheets, printed pp. from its Sommaire — the Grade 4 programme, corresponding to Books 26 and 30. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 30:** *المفيد في الرياضيات (Al Moufid fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذة والأستاذ* (teacher guide, Grade 4, Arabic; contents bilingual Arabic/French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 21,206,485 bytes, 357 PDF pages; 2019 edition. Its contents list the same 32 lessons as Books 26 and 29, with bilingual titles, read from rendered pages. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 31:** *المرجع في الرياضيات (Al Marjie fi al-Riyadiyat), دليل الأستاذ(ة)* (teacher guide, Grade 2, Arabic) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 55,959,139 bytes, 393 PDF pages. Items are the distinct rows of its yearly programme table (guide printed pp. 48–50, read from rendered pages), which states the Grade 2 content by domain with learning objectives — a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides, as the placements declare. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

**Book 32:** *Switch On! Every Day CE2, livret d'activités rituelles* (Grade 3, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 5,362,614 bytes, 109 PDF pages. Same series and standing as Book 9: the pupil booklet with answers, filed as a teacher edition. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 21 items (15 from student books, 6 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S2, Book T1, Book T2, Book S3, Book S4, Book T3, Book S5, Book T4, Book S6.
